**Topic:** Surface Area and Volume · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MEN-CYLV`

**Question:** Find the volume of a cylinder with radius 14 cm and height 5 cm. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `SOL-CYL-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SOL-CYL-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Volume = base area × height.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — V = πr²h. <!-- MQ-G10C29-F-CYLINDER_V-03-S1 -->

$$V = \frac{22}{7}\times 14^2\times 5$$

Step 2 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G10C29-F-CYLINDER_V-03-S2 -->

$$V = 3080\ \text{cm}^3$$

**Final answer:** $3080\ \text{cm}^3$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $3080\ \text{cm}^3$ · **Unit:** cm³

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q14", "source": "MOE2026", "source_type": "MOE_PRACTICE", "exam_year": null, "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 14, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G10C29", "pattern": "P1A-MENS", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** MOE Mathematics Practice Test 2025(2026) (English medium) — not a DoE exam paper · Paper I · Part A · Q14 · 2 marks · [MOE2026](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/68227/mod_resource/content/1/eol_mp_mat_2025%282-26%29.pdf)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C29", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MEN-CYLA", "diagram": "SOL-CYL-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S1", "MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S2", "MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q14"} -->
**Topic:** Surface Area and Volume · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-MEN-CYLA`

**Question:** Find the total surface area of a closed cylinder of radius 14 cm and height 6 cm. (π = 22/7)

**Diagram:** `SOL-CYL-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SOL-CYL-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Closed = two ends.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Curved surface = 2πrh. <!-- MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S1 -->

$$2\times\frac{22}{7}\times14\times6 = 528$$

Step 2 — Two circular ends = 2πr². <!-- MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S2 -->

$$2\times\frac{22}{7}\times14^2 = 1232$$

Step 3 — Add. <!-- MQ-G10C29-G-CYLINDER_T-01-S3 -->

$$528 + 1232 = 1760\ \text{cm}^2$$

**Final answer:** $1760\ \text{cm}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $1760\ \text{cm}^2$ · **Unit:** cm²

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>


## G10C30 — Probability

### PP-OL2019-PIA-Q20
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q20", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 20, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G10C30", "pattern": "P1A-PROB", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper I · Part A · Q20 · 2 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)
- **Topic (StudyBuddy summary):** Probability (marbles)
- **Question pattern:** `P1A-PROB` · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Official marking scheme:** not read (see register)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C30", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-BASIC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01-S1", "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q20"} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-PRO-BASIC`

**Question:** A bag has 5 red, 5 blue and 10 green balls (identical except colour). One ball is taken at random. Find the probability that it is red.

- **Hint 1:** Probability = favourable outcomes ÷ total outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total number of balls. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01-S1 -->

$$5+5+10 = 20$$

Step 2 — P(red) = red ÷ total. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-01-S2 -->

$$P = \frac{5}{20} = \frac{1}{4}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{1}{4}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{1}{4}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### PP-OL2018-PIA-Q13
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q13", "source": "OL2018", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2018", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 13, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G10C30", "pattern": "P1A-PROB", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C30", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-BASIC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-02-S1", "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-02-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q13"} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-PRO-BASIC`

**Question:** A bag has 4 red, 6 blue and 5 green balls (identical except colour). One ball is taken at random. Find the probability that it is red.

- **Hint 1:** Probability = favourable outcomes ÷ total outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total number of balls. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-02-S1 -->

$$4+6+5 = 15$$

Step 2 — P(red) = red ÷ total. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-02-S2 -->

$$P = \frac{4}{15} = \frac{4}{15}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{4}{15}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{4}{15}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-03", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C30", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-BASIC", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-03-S1", "MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-03-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIA-Q20"} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-PRO-BASIC`

**Question:** A bag has 2 red, 3 blue and 7 green balls (identical except colour). One ball is taken at random. Find the probability that it is red.

- **Hint 1:** Probability = favourable outcomes ÷ total outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Total number of balls. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-03-S1 -->

$$2+3+7 = 12$$

Step 2 — P(red) = red ÷ total. <!-- MQ-G10C30-F-PROB_SIMPL-03-S2 -->

$$P = \frac{2}{12} = \frac{1}{6}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{1}{6}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{1}{6}$

**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-PRO-BASIC`

**Question:** A bag has 3 red, 5 blue and 2 green balls (identical except colour). One ball is taken at random. Find the probability that it is red.

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C31", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-CENTRE", "diagram": "GEO-CIRC-CENTRE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-01-S1"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q06"} -->
**Topic:** Angles in a Circle · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CENTRE`

**Question:** O is the centre of the circle. The angle subtended by arc AB at a point C on the circumference is 42°. Find ∠AOB.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CIRC-CENTRE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CIRC-CENTRE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Both angles stand on the same arc AB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle at the centre = 2 × angle at the circumference. <!-- MQ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-01-S1 -->

$$\angle AOB = 2\times 42^\circ = 84^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $84^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $84^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C31", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-SEMI", "diagram": "GEO-CIRC-SEMI-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01-S1", "MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q19"} -->
**Topic:** Angles in a Circle · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-CIR-SEMI`

**Question:** AB is a diameter of a circle and C is a point on the circle. ∠CAB = 61°. Find ∠ABC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CIRC-SEMI-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CIRC-SEMI-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Which angle stands on the diameter?

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle in a semicircle is 90°. <!-- MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ACB = 90^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angle sum of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ABC = 180 - 90 - 61 = 29^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $29^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $29^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C31", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-SAMESEG", "diagram": "GEO-CIRC-SAMESEG-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01-S1", "MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q22"} -->
**Topic:** Angles in a Circle · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-SAMESEG`

**Question:** A, B, C, D are points on a circle (in that order). ∠BAC = 35° and ∠ACD = 50°. Find ∠BDC and ∠ABD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CIRC-SAMESEG-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CIRC-SAMESEG-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find which arc each angle stands on.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angles in the same segment (on arc BC) are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01-S1 -->

$$\angle BDC = \angle BAC = 35^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angles on arc AD are equal. <!-- MQ-G10C31-A-ANGLE_SAME-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ABD = \angle ACD = 50^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle BDC=35^\circ,\ \angle ABD=50^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle BDC=35^\circ,\ \angle ABD=50^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C32", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-SCALE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01-S1", "MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q19"} -->
**Topic:** Scale Diagrams · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-TRI-SCALE`

**Question:** On a scale diagram drawn to 1 : 100, a length is 7 cm. Find the actual length in metres.

- **Hint 1:** 1 cm on paper = scale cm in reality.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply by the scale. <!-- MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01-S1 -->

$$7\times 100 = 700\ \text{cm}$$

Step 2 — Convert cm to m (÷ 100). <!-- MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01-S2 -->

$$700\div 100 = 7\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $7\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $7\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-02", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C32", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-SCALE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-02-S1", "MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-02-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIA-Q13"} -->
**Topic:** Scale Diagrams · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-TRI-SCALE`

**Question:** On a scale diagram drawn to 1 : 2000, a length is 3.2 cm. Find the actual length in metres.

- **Hint 1:** 1 cm on paper = scale cm in reality.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Multiply by the scale. <!-- MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-02-S1 -->

$$3.2\times 2000 = 6400\ \text{cm}$$

Step 2 — Convert cm to m (÷ 100). <!-- MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-02-S2 -->

$$6400\div 100 = 64\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $64\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $64\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C01", "area": "NUM", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-NUM-REAL", "diagram": null, "kind": "concept", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01-S1", "MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Real Numbers · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-NUM-REAL`

**Question:** Is $\sqrt{7}$ rational or irrational? Give a reason.

- **Hint 1:** √ of a non-square whole number is irrational.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — A rational number can be written as p/q (q ≠ 0); its decimal ends or repeats. <!-- MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{p}{q}$$

Step 2 — Check the given number. <!-- MQ-G11C01-F-CLASSIFY-01-S2 -->

$$\sqrt{7} \Rightarrow \text{irrational}$$

**Final answer:** $\text{irrational}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>


## G11C05 — Volume of Solids

### PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q12
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q12", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "B", "question": 12, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C05", "pattern": "P2B-VOL", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper II · Part B · Q12 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)
- **Topic (StudyBuddy summary):** Hemisphere and prism volume
- **Question pattern:** `P2B-VOL` · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Official marking scheme:** not read (see register)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C05", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-SPHV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S1", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S2", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q12"} -->
**Topic:** Volume of Solids · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SPHV`

**Question:** A solid metal sphere of radius 9 cm is melted and recast into small spheres of radius 3 cm. How many small spheres are made?

- **Hint 1:** You don't need π — it cancels.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Volume is conserved: N × small volume = big volume. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S1 -->

$$N\times\frac43\pi(3)^3 = \frac43\pi(9)^3$$

Step 2 — Cancel (4/3)π on both sides. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S2 -->

$$N\times 27 = 729$$

Step 3 — Divide. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-01-S3 -->

$$N = 729\div 27 = 27$$

**Final answer:** $27$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $27$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C05", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-SPHV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S1", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S2", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q09"} -->
**Topic:** Volume of Solids · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SPHV`

**Question:** A solid metal sphere of radius 10 cm is melted and recast into small spheres of radius 2 cm. How many small spheres are made?

- **Hint 1:** You don't need π — it cancels.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Volume is conserved: N × small volume = big volume. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S1 -->

$$N\times\frac43\pi(2)^3 = \frac43\pi(10)^3$$

Step 2 — Cancel (4/3)π on both sides. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S2 -->

$$N\times 8 = 1000$$

Step 3 — Divide. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-02-S3 -->

$$N = 1000\div 8 = 125$$

**Final answer:** $125$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $125$

### PP-MOE2026-PIIB-Q10

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C05", "area": "MEN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MEN-SPHV", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S1", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S2", "MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIB-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Volume of Solids · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-MEN-SPHV`

**Question:** A solid metal sphere of radius 6 cm is melted and recast into small spheres of radius 2 cm. How many small spheres are made?

- **Hint 1:** You don't need π — it cancels.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Volume is conserved: N × small volume = big volume. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S1 -->

$$N\times\frac43\pi(2)^3 = \frac43\pi(6)^3$$

Step 2 — Cancel (4/3)π on both sides. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S2 -->

$$N\times 8 = 216$$

Step 3 — Divide. <!-- MQ-G11C05-A-MELT_SPHER-03-S3 -->

$$N = 216\div 8 = 27$$

**Final answer:** $27$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $27$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>


## G11C07 — Algebraic Fractions

### PP-OL2019-PIA-Q09
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q09", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 9, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G11C07", "pattern": "P1A-AFRAC", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper I · Part A · Q9 · 2 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)
- **Topic (StudyBuddy summary):** Simplifying an algebraic fraction

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C07", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01-S1", "MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q09"} -->
**Topic:** Algebraic Fractions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-AFRAC`

**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x^2 - 16}{x + 4}$

- **Hint 1:** You may cancel factors, never terms.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise the numerator. <!-- MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{(x+4)(x-4)}{x+4}$$

Step 2 — Cancel the common factor. <!-- MQ-G11C07-F-AF_CANCEL-01-S2 -->

$$= x - 4$$

**Final answer:** $x - 4$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

## G11C08 — Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines

### PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q09
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q09", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "B", "question": 9, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C08", "pattern": "P2B-PGEO", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper II · Part B · Q9 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)
- **Topic (StudyBuddy summary):** Parallel lines and triangle proofs
- **Question pattern:** `P2B-PGEO` · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Official marking scheme:** not read (see register)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "diagram": "GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S1", "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S2", "MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q09"} -->
**Topic:** Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-AREA-PAR`

**Question:** In parallelogram ABCD, E is any point on DC. Prove that area ΔAEB = area ΔADE + area ΔBCE.

**Diagram:** `GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-PARALLEL-AREA-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Compare each part with the whole parallelogram.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — ΔAEB and parallelogram ABCD: same base AB, between AB ∥ DC. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S1 -->

$$\triangle AEB = \tfrac12\,ABCD$$

Step 2 — The rest of the parallelogram is the other half. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S2 -->

$$\triangle ADE + \triangle BCE = ABCD - \triangle AEB = \tfrac12\,ABCD$$

Step 3 — Both equal half the parallelogram. <!-- MQ-G11C08-A-AREA_PROOF-01-S3 -->

$$\therefore \triangle AEB = \triangle ADE + \triangle BCE$$

**Final answer:** $\triangle AEB = \triangle ADE + \triangle BCE$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q01"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-RATES`

**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 40 000. The council charges rates of 10%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **Hint 1:** Rates are a percentage of the ANNUAL value, paid in four quarters.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Annual rates. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S1 -->

$$40\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 4000$$

Step 2 — One quarter = ÷ 4. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-01-S2 -->

$$4000\div 4 = 1000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }1000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }1000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-RATES`

**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 24 000. The council charges rates of 8%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **Hint 1:** Rates are a percentage of the ANNUAL value, paid in four quarters.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Annual rates. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-02-S1 -->

$$24\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 1920$$

Step 2 — One quarter = ÷ 4. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-02-S2 -->

$$1920\div 4 = 480$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }480$

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S2", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S3", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S4", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIB-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 100 000 is deposited at 8% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S1 -->

$$100\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S2 -->

$$100\,000 + 8000 = 108\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S3 -->

$$108\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8640$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S4 -->

$$108\,000 + 8640 = 116\,640$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01-S5 -->

$$116\,640 - 100\,000 = 16\,640$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Calculating year-2 interest on the original amount (that is simple interest).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S1", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S2", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S3", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S4", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIB-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 40 000 is deposited at 5% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S1 -->

$$40\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S2 -->

$$40\,000 + 2000 = 42\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S3 -->

$$42\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2100$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S4 -->

$$42\,000 + 2100 = 44\,100$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02-S5 -->

$$44\,100 - 40\,000 = 4100$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Calculating year-2 interest on the original amount (that is simple interest).

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-TAX", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S1", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S2", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S3", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S4", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIIA-Q01"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-TAX`

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 2 400 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S1 -->

$$2\,400\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 1\,200\,000$$

Step 2 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 6%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S2 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 30\,000$$

Step 3 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 12%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S3 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{12}{100} = 60\,000$$

Step 4 — Tax on Rs. 200 000 at 18%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S4 -->

$$200\,000\times\frac{18}{100} = 36\,000$$

Step 5 — Total tax. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-01-S5 -->

$$30\,000 + 60\,000 + 36\,000 = 126\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }126\,000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }126\,000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Applying the highest rate to the whole income.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04-S1", "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIA-Q01"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-FIN-RATES`

**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 36 000. The council charges rates of 6%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **Hint 1:** Rates are a percentage of the ANNUAL value, paid in four quarters.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Annual rates. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04-S1 -->

$$36\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 2160$$

Step 2 — One quarter = ÷ 4. <!-- MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04-S2 -->

$$2160\div 4 = 540$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }540$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }540$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-TAX`

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 1 500 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-02-S1 -->

$$1\,500\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 300\,000$$

$$300\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 18\,000$$

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S1", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S2", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S3", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S4", "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 50 000 is deposited at 10% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S1 -->

$$50\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 5000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S2 -->

$$50\,000 + 5000 = 55\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S3 -->

$$55\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 5500$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S4 -->

$$55\,000 + 5500 = 60\,500$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03-S5 -->

$$60\,500 - 50\,000 = 10\,500$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }60\,500,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }10\,500$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }60\,500,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }10\,500$ · **Unit:** Rs.

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Calculating year-2 interest on the original amount (that is simple interest).

**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-CI`

**Question:** Rs. 80 000 is deposited at 10% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **Hint 1:** Compound: each year's interest is added before the next year.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Interest for year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-04-S1 -->

$$80\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 8000$$

Step 2 — Amount after year 1. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-04-S2 -->

$$80\,000 + 8000 = 88\,000$$

Step 3 — Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-04-S3 -->

$$88\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 8800$$

Step 4 — Amount after year 2. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-04-S4 -->

$$88\,000 + 8800 = 96\,800$$

Step 5 — Total interest. <!-- MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-04-S5 -->

$$96\,800 - 80\,000 = 16\,800$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }96\,800,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,800$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }96\,800,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,800$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C09", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-TAX", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S1", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S2", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S3", "MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q01"} -->
**Topic:** Percentage · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-TAX`

**Question:** (Illustrative tax table) The first Rs. 1 200 000 of annual income is tax-free. The next Rs. 500 000 is taxed at 6%, the next Rs. 500 000 at 12%, and the rest at 18%. Find the income tax on an annual income of Rs. 1 800 000.

- **Hint 1:** Tax each band separately, then add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Taxable income. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S1 -->

$$1\,800\,000 - 1\,200\,000 = 600\,000$$

Step 2 — Tax on Rs. 500 000 at 6%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S2 -->

$$500\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 30\,000$$

Step 3 — Tax on Rs. 100 000 at 12%. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S3 -->

$$100\,000\times\frac{12}{100} = 12\,000$$

Step 4 — Total tax. <!-- MQ-G11C09-A-INCOME_TAX-03-S4 -->

$$30\,000 + 12\,000 = 42\,000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }42\,000$

<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q01", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "A", "question": 1, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C10", "pattern": "P2A-FIN", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper II · Part A · Q1 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C10", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-FIN-SHARE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q01"} -->
**Topic:** Share Market · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SHARE`

**Question:** Kamal has Rs. 90 000. Option A: buy shares at Rs. 45 each paying an annual dividend of Rs. 4 per share. Option B: deposit in a bank at 9% simple interest per year. Which gives the higher income in one year, and by how much?

- **Hint 1:** Find both incomes, then subtract.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Number of shares. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S1 -->

$$90\,000\div 45 = 2000$$

Step 2 — Dividend income. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S2 -->

$$2000\times4 = 8000$$

Step 3 — Bank interest for one year. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S3 -->

$$90\,000\times\frac{9}{100} = 8100$$

Step 4 — Compare. <!-- MQ-G11C10-A-SHARES_VS_-01-S4 -->

$$|8000 - 8100| = 100$$

**Final answer:** $\text{bank},\ \text{Rs. }100$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{bank},\ \text{Rs. }100$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C10", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-SHARE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-01-S1"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIB-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Share Market · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SHARE`

**Question:** A person owns 1200 shares. The company pays an annual dividend of Rs. 5 per share. Find the annual dividend income.

- **Hint 1:** Dividend is paid per share.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Dividend income = shares × dividend per share. <!-- MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-01-S1 -->

$$1200\times 5 = 6000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }6000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }6000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C10", "area": "FIN", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-SHARE", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-02-S1"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIB-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Share Market · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-FIN-SHARE`

**Question:** A person owns 2000 shares. The company pays an annual dividend of Rs. 3 per share. Find the annual dividend income.

- **Hint 1:** Dividend is paid per share.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Dividend income = shares × dividend per share. <!-- MQ-G11C10-G-DIVIDEND_I-02-S1 -->

$$2000\times 3 = 6000$$

**Final answer:** $\text{Rs. }6000$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\text{Rs. }6000$ · **Unit:** Rs.

## G11C11 — Mid-point Theorem

### PP-OL2019-PIA-Q10
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q10", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 10, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G11C11", "pattern": "P1A-GEO", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper I · Part A · Q10 · 2 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)
- **Topic (StudyBuddy summary):** Right triangle with midpoints
- **Question pattern:** `P1A-GEO` · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Official marking scheme:** not read (see register)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C11", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S1", "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Mid-point Theorem · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-MID`

**Question:** In ΔABC, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and AC. BC = 10 cm. Find PQ.

**Diagram:** `GEO-MIDPT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-MIDPT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Join of midpoints = half the third side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Mid-point theorem: PQ ∥ BC and PQ = ½ BC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S1 -->

$$PQ = \tfrac12 BC$$

Step 2 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-01-S2 -->

$$PQ = \tfrac12\times 10 = 5\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $5\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $5\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

### PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q10
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q10", "source": "MOE2024", "source_type": "MOE_PRACTICE", "exam_year": null, "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "B", "question": 10, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C11", "pattern": "P2B-PGEO", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** MOE Mathematics Practice Test 2023(2024) (English medium) — not a DoE exam paper · Paper II · Part B · Q10 · 10 marks · [MOE2024](https://www.e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/moodle/pluginfile.php/460944/mod_resource/content/1/2023%282024%29_Mathematics_Practice_Paper_MOE_EM.docx.pdf)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C11", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S1", "MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Mid-point Theorem · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-MID`

**Question:** In ΔABC, D is the midpoint of AB. A line through D parallel to BC meets AC at E. If AC = 14 cm and BC = 10 cm, find AE and DE. Give reasons.

**Diagram:** `GEO-MIDPT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-MIDPT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** A line from a midpoint parallel to a side bisects the third side.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Converse of the mid-point theorem: E is the midpoint of AC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S1 -->

$$AE = \tfrac12 AC = 7\ \text{cm}$$

Step 2 — Then DE = ½ BC. <!-- MQ-G11C11-A-MIDPOINT_C-01-S2 -->

$$DE = \tfrac12\times 10 = 5\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $AE = 7\text{ cm},\ DE = 5\text{ cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $AE = 7\text{ cm},\ DE = 5\text{ cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper II · Part A · Q2 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q02"} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} + 4x + 1$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{4}{2} = 2$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S2 -->

$$y = (x + 2)^2 - (2)^2  + 1$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S3 -->

$$y = (x + 2)^2 - 3$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-01-S4 -->

$$(-2,\ -3),\quad x < -2$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x + 2)^2 - 3;\ (-2,-3);\ x<-2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x + 2)^2 - 3;\ (-2,-3);\ x<-2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIIA-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} - 4x - 1$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S1 -->

$$\frac{-4}{2} = -2$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S2 -->

$$y = (x - 2)^2 - (-2)^2  - 1$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S3 -->

$$y = (x - 2)^2 - 5$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-02-S4 -->

$$(2,\ -5),\quad x < 2$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x - 2)^2 - 5;\ (2,-5);\ x<2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x - 2)^2 - 5;\ (2,-5);\ x<2$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S1", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S2", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S3", "MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIA-Q02"} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-GRA-VERTEX`

**Question:** Express $y = x^{2} + 2x - 2$ in the form $y = (x + a)^2 + k$. Hence write the turning point and the range of x for which y is decreasing.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** x² + bx = (x + b/2)² − (b/2)².

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Half the x-coefficient. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S1 -->

$$\frac{2}{2} = 1$$

Step 2 — Complete the square. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S2 -->

$$y = (x + 1)^2 - (1)^2  - 2$$

Step 3 — Simplify the constant. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S3 -->

$$y = (x + 1)^2 - 3$$

Step 4 — Turning point and decreasing range. <!-- MQ-G11C12-A-COMPLETE_S-03-S4 -->

$$(-1,\ -3),\quad x < -1$$

**Final answer:** $y=(x + 1)^2 - 3;\ (-1,-3);\ x<-1$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $y=(x + 1)^2 - 3;\ (-1,-3);\ x<-1$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q04
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q04", "source": "MOE2026", "source_type": "MOE_PRACTICE", "exam_year": null, "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "A", "question": 4, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C12", "pattern": "P2A-QGRAPH", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** MOE Mathematics Practice Test 2025(2026) (English medium) — not a DoE exam paper · Paper II · Part A · Q4 · 10 marks · [MOE2026](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/68227/mod_resource/content/1/eol_mp_mat_2025%282-26%29.pdf)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C12", "area": "GRA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S1", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S2", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S3", "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q04"} -->
**Topic:** Graphs · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-GRA-QUAD`

**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 2x - 3$, prepare a table of values for x from -2 to 4, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 2x - 3 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

**Diagram:** `GRA-QUAD-01` → `Shared_Assets/Graphs/GRA-QUAD-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for y = 0 in your table.
- **Hint 2:** The curve is symmetric about the turning point.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Table of values. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S1 -->

$$\begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array}$$

Step 2 — Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S2 -->

$$x = -1,\ x = 3$$

Step 3 — Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-1+3}{2} = 1$$

Step 4 — Substitute to get y. <!-- MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01-S4 -->

$$y = -4 \Rightarrow (1,\ -4)$$

**Final answer:** $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>


## G11C13 — Equations

### PP-OL2019-PIA-Q11
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q11", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 11, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G11C13", "pattern": "P1A-QEQ", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper I · Part A · Q11 · 2 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q11"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} - 7x + 10 = 0$

- **Hint 1:** If A × B = 0, then A = 0 or B = 0.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S1 -->

$$(x  - 2)(x  - 5) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S2 -->

$$x  - 2 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  - 5 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01-S3 -->

$$x = 2\ \text{or}\ x = 5$$

**Final answer:** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

### PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q04
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q04", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "II", "part": "A", "question": 4, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C13", "pattern": "P2A-QEQ", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper II · Part A · Q4 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q04"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 3 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 54 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S1 -->

$$x(x+3) = 54$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 3x - 54 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S3 -->

$$(x-6)(x+9) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 6$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S4 -->

$$(6+2)\times(9+2) = 88$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-01-S5 -->

$$88 - 54 = 34\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=6\text{ m},\ \text{path}=34\text{ m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=6\text{ m},\ \text{path}=34\text{ m}^2$ · **Unit:** m

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S1", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S2", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} + 5x - 6 = 0$

- **Hint 1:** If A × B = 0, then A = 0 or B = 0.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S1 -->

$$(x  - 1)(x  + 6) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S2 -->

$$x  - 1 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  + 6 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02-S3 -->

$$x = 1\ \text{or}\ x = -6$$

**Final answer:** $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIIA-Q02"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 3 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 88 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S1 -->

$$x(x+3) = 88$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 3x - 88 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S3 -->

$$(x-8)(x+11) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 8$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S4 -->

$$(8+2)\times(11+2) = 130$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-02-S5 -->

$$130 - 88 = 42\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=8\text{ m},\ \text{path}=42\text{ m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=8\text{ m},\ \text{path}=42\text{ m}^2$ · **Unit:** m

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S1", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S2", "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIA-Q24"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} + 7x + 10 = 0$

- **Hint 1:** If A × B = 0, then A = 0 or B = 0.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Factorise. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S1 -->

$$(x  + 2)(x  + 5) = 0$$

Step 2 — Each factor = 0. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S2 -->

$$x  + 2 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  + 5 = 0$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03-S3 -->

$$x = -2\ \text{or}\ x = -5$$

**Final answer:** $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S1", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S2", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S3", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S4", "MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIA-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QUAD`

**Question:** A rectangular garden is 2 m longer than it is wide. A path 1 m wide is laid all around the OUTSIDE, and the area of the garden alone is 24 m². Find the width of the garden and the area of the path.

- **Hint 1:** A 1 m path on both sides adds 2 m to each dimension.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Let width = x, length = x + d. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S1 -->

$$x(x+2) = 24$$

Step 2 — Rearrange. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S2 -->

$$x^2 + 2x - 24 = 0$$

Step 3 — Factorise and reject the negative root. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S3 -->

$$(x-4)(x+6) = 0 \Rightarrow x = 4$$

Step 4 — Outer rectangle (add 2 m to each dimension). <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S4 -->

$$(4+2)\times(6+2) = 48$$

Step 5 — Path = outer − garden. <!-- MQ-G11C13-A-QUAD_WORD-03-S5 -->

$$48 - 24 = 24\ \text{m}^2$$

**Final answer:** $x=4\text{ m},\ \text{path}=24\text{ m}^2$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=4\text{ m},\ \text{path}=24\text{ m}^2$ · **Unit:** m

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C13", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-QFORM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S1", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S2", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S3", "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Equations · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-ALG-QFORM`

**Question:** Solve $x^{2} + 2x - 4 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **Hint 1:** x = [−b ± √(b² − 4ac)] / 2a
- **Hint 2:** Be careful with −b when b is negative.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Identify a, b, c. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S1 -->

$$a = 1,\ b = 2,\ c = -4$$

Step 2 — Discriminant b² − 4ac. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S2 -->

$$(2)^2 - 4(1)(-4) = 20$$

Step 3 — Formula. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S3 -->

$$x = \frac{-(2) \pm \sqrt{20}}{2\times1},\quad \sqrt{20} \approx 4.472$$

Step 4 — Two answers. <!-- MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01-S4 -->

$$x \approx 1.24\ \text{or}\ x \approx -3.24$$

**Final answer:** $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$ · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.01

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

- **Source:** MOE Mathematics Practice Test 2025(2026) (English medium) — not a DoE exam paper · Paper II · Part B · Q9 · 10 marks · [MOE2026](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/68227/mod_resource/content/1/eol_mp_mat_2025%282-26%29.pdf)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C14", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-CHORDS-INT-01", "kind": "proof", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S1", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S2", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S3", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S4", "MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIB-Q09"} -->
**Topic:** Equiangular Triangles · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-SIM`

**Question:** AB and CD are two chords of a circle that intersect at X inside the circle. Prove that ΔAXC and ΔDXB are equiangular and hence that AX × XB = CX × XD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CHORDS-INT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CHORDS-INT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Look for vertically opposite angles and same-segment angles.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Vertically opposite angles. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S1 -->

$$\angle AXC = \angle DXB$$

Step 2 — Angles in the same segment (on arc BC... arc AD). <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ACX = \angle ACD = \angle ABD = \angle DBX$$

Step 3 — Third angles are equal (angle sum). <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S3 -->

$$\angle CAX = \angle BDX$$

Step 4 — So the triangles are equiangular; sides are proportional. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{AX}{DX} = \frac{CX}{BX}$$

Step 5 — Cross-multiply. <!-- MQ-G11C14-A-SIMILAR_PR-01-S5 -->

$$AX\times XB = CX\times XD$$

**Final answer:** $AX\times XB = CX\times XD$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-STA-MEDIAN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S1", "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S2", "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q23"} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-STA-MEDIAN`

**Question:** Find the median of: 18, 21, 22, 23, 25, 53, 56

- **Hint 1:** Always order the data first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — The data is already in order. Count the values. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S1 -->

$$n = 7$$

Step 2 — Median position = (n + 1)/2. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{7+1}{2} = 4$$

Step 3 — Read that value. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-01-S3 -->

$$\text{median} = 23$$

**Final answer:** $23$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $23$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S1", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S2", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIB-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-STA-QUART`

**Question:** For the 11 values 6, 28, 29, 30, 32, 38, 43, 48, 51, 59, 74, find the lower quartile, the upper quartile and the interquartile range.

- **Hint 1:** Positions: (n+1)/4 and 3(n+1)/4.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Q₁ position = (n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S1 -->

$$\frac{11+1}{4} = 3 \Rightarrow Q_1 = 29$$

Step 2 — Q₃ position = 3(n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S2 -->

$$\frac{3(11+1)}{4} = 9 \Rightarrow Q_3 = 51$$

Step 3 — Interquartile range = Q₃ − Q₁. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01-S3 -->

$$51 - 29 = 22$$

**Final answer:** $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S1", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S2", "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIB-Q04"} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-STA-QUART`

**Question:** For the 11 values 13, 17, 22, 28, 36, 47, 48, 55, 63, 73, 74, find the lower quartile, the upper quartile and the interquartile range.

- **Hint 1:** Positions: (n+1)/4 and 3(n+1)/4.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Q₁ position = (n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S1 -->

$$\frac{11+1}{4} = 3 \Rightarrow Q_1 = 22$$

Step 2 — Q₃ position = 3(n + 1)/4. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{3(11+1)}{4} = 9 \Rightarrow Q_3 = 63$$

Step 3 — Interquartile range = Q₃ − Q₁. <!-- MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02-S3 -->

$$63 - 22 = 41$$

**Final answer:** $Q_1=22,\ Q_3=63,\ \text{IQR}=41$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $Q_1=22,\ Q_3=63,\ \text{IQR}=41$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C15", "area": "STA", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-STA-MEDIAN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S1", "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S2", "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q12"} -->
**Topic:** Data Representation and Prediction · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-STA-MEDIAN`

**Question:** Find the median of: 13, 21, 35, 37, 44, 49, 58

- **Hint 1:** Always order the data first.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — The data is already in order. Count the values. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S1 -->

$$n = 7$$

Step 2 — Median position = (n + 1)/2. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S2 -->

$$\frac{7+1}{2} = 4$$

Step 3 — Read that value. <!-- MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02-S3 -->

$$\text{median} = 37$$

**Final answer:** $37$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $37$

### PP-OL2019-PIA-Q15
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q15", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 15, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G11C16", "pattern": "P1A-PROG", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q15"} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 5th term of the geometric progression 2, 6, 18, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S1 -->

$$r = 3$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S2 -->

$$T_{5} = 2\times(3)^{4}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01-S3 -->

$$= 2\times 81 = 162$$

**Final answer:** $162$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $162$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C16", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S1", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S2", "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIA-Q21"} -->
**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 7th term of the geometric progression 5, 10, 20, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S1 -->

$$r = 2$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S2 -->

$$T_{7} = 5\times(2)^{6}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-02-S3 -->

$$= 5\times 64 = 320$$

**Final answer:** $320$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $320$

**Topic:** Geometric Progressions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-GP-TN`

**Question:** Find the 4th term of the geometric progression 1, 4, 16, …

- **Hint 1:** Multiply by r each time, not add.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Common ratio r = second ÷ first. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S1 -->

$$r = 4$$

Step 2 — Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S2 -->

$$T_{4} = 1\times(4)^{3}$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-03-S3 -->

$$= 1\times 64 = 64$$

**Final answer:** $64$

### PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q16
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q16", "source": "MOE2026", "source_type": "MOE_PRACTICE", "exam_year": null, "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "A", "question": 16, "marks": 2, "chapter": "G11C17", "pattern": "P1A-GEO", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C17", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S1", "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q16"} -->
**Topic:** Pythagoras' Theorem · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-PYTH`

**Question:** The two shorter sides of a right-angled triangle are 6 cm and 8 cm. Find the hypotenuse.

**Diagram:** `GEO-RIGHT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-RIGHT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The hypotenuse is opposite the right angle.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — c² = a² + b². <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S1 -->

$$c^2 = 6^2 + 8^2 = 36 + 64 = 100$$

Step 2 — Square root. <!-- MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01-S2 -->

$$c = \sqrt{100} = 10\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $10\ \text{cm}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $10\ \text{cm}$ · **Unit:** cm

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-DEPR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S2", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S3", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIA-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From the top of a 50 m cliff, the angles of depression of two boats in line with the foot of the cliff are 35° and 60°. Find the distance between the boats. (tan 35° = 0.7002, tan 60° = 1.7321)

**Diagram:** `TRI-DEPR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-DEPR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The smaller angle belongs to the farther boat.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle of depression = angle of elevation from the boat (alternate angles). <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S1 -->

$$\tan\theta = \frac{50}{x}$$

Step 2 — Far boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S2 -->

$$x_1 = \frac{50}{\tan 35^\circ} = \frac{50}{0.7002} \approx 71.41$$

Step 3 — Near boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S3 -->

$$x_2 = \frac{50}{1.7321} \approx 28.87$$

Step 4 — Difference. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-01-S4 -->

$$71.41 - 28.87 \approx 42.54\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $\approx 42.54\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\approx 42.54\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.1

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-DEPR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S1", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S2", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S3", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIIB-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From the top of a 40 m cliff, the angles of depression of two boats in line with the foot of the cliff are 30° and 50°. Find the distance between the boats. (tan 30° = 0.5774, tan 50° = 1.1918)

**Diagram:** `TRI-DEPR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-DEPR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The smaller angle belongs to the farther boat.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle of depression = angle of elevation from the boat (alternate angles). <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S1 -->

$$\tan\theta = \frac{40}{x}$$

Step 2 — Far boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S2 -->

$$x_1 = \frac{40}{\tan 30^\circ} = \frac{40}{0.5774} \approx 69.28$$

Step 3 — Near boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S3 -->

$$x_2 = \frac{40}{1.1918} \approx 33.56$$

Step 4 — Difference. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-02-S4 -->

$$69.28 - 33.56 \approx 35.71\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $\approx 35.71\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\approx 35.71\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.1

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-DEPR-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S1", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S2", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S3", "MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIA-Q04"} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From the top of a 60 m cliff, the angles of depression of two boats in line with the foot of the cliff are 25° and 45°. Find the distance between the boats. (tan 25° = 0.4663, tan 45° = 1.0000)

**Diagram:** `TRI-DEPR-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-DEPR-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The smaller angle belongs to the farther boat.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angle of depression = angle of elevation from the boat (alternate angles). <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S1 -->

$$\tan\theta = \frac{60}{x}$$

Step 2 — Far boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S2 -->

$$x_1 = \frac{60}{\tan 25^\circ} = \frac{60}{0.4663} \approx 128.67$$

Step 3 — Near boat. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S3 -->

$$x_2 = \frac{60}{1.0000} \approx 60.00$$

Step 4 — Difference. <!-- MQ-G11C18-A-TWO_ANGLES-03-S4 -->

$$128.67 - 60.00 \approx 68.67\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $\approx 68.67\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\approx 68.67\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.1
