#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q17"} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** In right-angled ΔABC, ∠B = 90°, AB = 4 cm, BC = 3 cm and AC = 5 cm. Write sin A, cos A and tan A.

**Diagram:** `GEO-RIGHT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-RIGHT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** SOH CAH TOA.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — For angle A: opposite = BC, adjacent = AB, hypotenuse = AC. <!-- MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S1 -->

$$\text{opp}=BC,\ \text{adj}=AB,\ \text{hyp}=AC$$

Step 2 — Ratios. <!-- MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-01-S2 -->

$$\sin A = \frac{3}{5},\ \cos A = \frac{4}{5},\ \tan A = \frac{3}{4}$$

**Final answer:** $\sin A=\frac{3}{5},\ \cos A=\frac{4}{5},\ \tan A=\frac{3}{4}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\sin A=\frac{3}{5},\ \cos A=\frac{4}{5},\ \tan A=\frac{3}{4}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C18", "area": "TRI", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-ELEV-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIA-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Trigonometry · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-TRI-RATIOS`

**Question:** From a point 20 m from the foot of a vertical tower on level ground, the angle of elevation of the top is 35°. Find the height of the tower. (Use tables: tan 35° = 0.7002)

**Diagram:** `TRI-ELEV-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/TRI-ELEV-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Opposite and adjacent → tan.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw a right triangle: height opposite the angle, distance adjacent. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S1 -->

$$\tan 35^\circ = \frac{h}{20}$$

Step 2 — Make h the subject. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S2 -->

$$h = 20\times\tan 35^\circ = 20\times 0.7002$$

Step 3 — Calculate. <!-- MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01-S3 -->

$$h \approx 14.00\ \text{m}$$

**Final answer:** $14.00\ \text{m}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $14.00\ \text{m}$ · **Unit:** m · **Accepted tolerance:** ±0.05

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MAT-ADD", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S1", "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q22"} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}-1 & 2\\0 & 4\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}2 & 2\\-2 & 2\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}-1+(2) & 2+(2)\\0+(-2) & 4+(2)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-01-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}1 & 4\\-2 & 6\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}1 & 4\\-2 & 6\end{pmatrix}$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}1 & -3\\5 & 4\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}4 & 4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-02-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}1+(4) & -3+(4)\\5+(4) & 4+(2)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-02-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}5 & 1\\9 & 6\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}5 & 1\\9 & 6\end{pmatrix}$

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C19", "area": "ALG", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-MAT-ADD", "diagram": null, "kind": "algebra", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S1", "MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q24"} -->
**Topic:** Matrices · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-MAT-ADD`

**Question:** Find A + B where $A = \begin{pmatrix}3 & -1\\5 & 5\end{pmatrix}$ and $B = \begin{pmatrix}4 & -3\\-1 & -3\end{pmatrix}$.

- **Hint 1:** Matrices of the same order are added element by element.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Add corresponding elements. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S1 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}3+(4) & -1+(-3)\\5+(-1) & 5+(-3)\end{pmatrix}$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C19-F-MAT_ADD-03-S2 -->

$$\begin{pmatrix}7 & -4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$$

**Final answer:** $\begin{pmatrix}7 & -4\\4 & 2\end{pmatrix}$

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S1", "MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q13"} -->
**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. ∠A = 70° and ∠B = 95°. Find ∠C and ∠D.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Opposite angles are supplementary.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S1 -->

$$\angle C = 180 - 70 = 110^\circ$$

Step 2 — Same for B and D. <!-- MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01-S2 -->

$$\angle D = 180 - 95 = 85^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C21", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S1", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S2", "MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q10"} -->
**Topic:** Cyclic Quadrilaterals · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-CYCLIC`

**Question:** In cyclic quadrilateral PQRS, ∠P = $(2x + 10)^\circ$ and ∠R = $(x + 95)^\circ$. Find x and ∠R.

**Diagram:** `GEO-CYCLIC-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-CYCLIC-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** P and R are opposite.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Opposite angles add to 180°. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + (x+95) = 180$$

Step 2 — Simplify. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S2 -->

$$3x + 105 = 180 \Rightarrow 3x = 75$$

Step 3 — Solve and substitute. <!-- MQ-G11C21-A-CYCLIC_ALG-01-S3 -->

$$x = 25,\ \angle R = 25+95 = 120^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=25,\ \angle R=120^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=25,\ \angle R=120^\circ$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-ALTSEG", "diagram": "GEO-ALTSEG-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIIB-Q12"} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-ALTSEG`

**Question:** TA is a tangent to a circle at A. AB and AC are chords. ∠TAB = 55° and ∠ABC = 65°. Find ∠ACB and ∠BAC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-ALTSEG-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ALTSEG-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the angle on the OTHER side of the chord AB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Alternate segment theorem: angle between tangent and chord = angle in the alternate segment. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ACB = \angle TAB = 55^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angle sum of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-01-S2 -->

$$\angle BAC = 180 - 55 - 65 = 60^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle ACB=55^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle ACB=55^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C22", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-CIR-ALTSEG", "diagram": "GEO-ALTSEG-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S1", "MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q12"} -->
**Topic:** Tangents · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-CIR-ALTSEG`

**Question:** TA is a tangent to a circle at A. AB and AC are chords. ∠TAB = 40° and ∠ABC = 80°. Find ∠ACB and ∠BAC.

**Diagram:** `GEO-ALTSEG-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-ALTSEG-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Find the angle on the OTHER side of the chord AB.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Alternate segment theorem: angle between tangent and chord = angle in the alternate segment. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S1 -->

$$\angle ACB = \angle TAB = 40^\circ$$

Step 2 — Angle sum of ΔABC. <!-- MQ-G11C22-A-ALTERNATE_-02-S2 -->

$$\angle BAC = 180 - 40 - 80 = 60^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $\angle ACB=40^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\angle ACB=40^\circ,\ \angle BAC=60^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "C-INCIRCLE", "diagram": "CON-ANGBIS-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIIB-Q08"} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `C-INCIRCLE`

**Question:** Construct an equilateral triangle of side 6 cm and construct its inscribed circle (incircle). Write the steps and measure the radius.

**Diagram:** `CON-ANGBIS-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-ANGBIS-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The incentre is where the angle bisectors meet.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw AB = 6 cm; arcs of 6 cm from A and B meet at C. <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$AB = BC = CA = 6$$

Step 2 — Bisect two angles (e.g. ∠A and ∠B). <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$\text{bisectors meet at } I$$

Step 3 — Draw a perpendicular from I to a side; this is the radius. <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$r = ID$$

Step 4 — Expected radius (check by calculation). <!-- MQ-G11C23-A-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$r = \frac{6}{2\sqrt3} \approx 1.73\ \text{cm}$$

**Final answer:** $r\approx 1.7\ \text{cm}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "C-CIRCUM", "diagram": "CON-CIRCUM-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2024-PIIB-Q08"} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `C-CIRCUM`

**Question:** Construct triangle ABC with AB = 6 cm, BC = 7 cm and AC = 5 cm, then construct its circumcircle. Write the steps.

**Diagram:** `CON-CIRCUM-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-CIRCUM-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** The circumcentre is equidistant from all three vertices.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw BC = 7 cm. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$BC = 7$$

Step 2 — Arc radius 6 cm from B and arc radius 5 cm from C meet at A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$A$$

Step 3 — Construct the perpendicular bisectors of two sides. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$\text{bisectors of }AB,\ BC$$

Step 4 — They meet at O (circumcentre). Draw the circle with radius OA. <!-- MQ-G11C23-G-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$OA = OB = OC$$

**Final answer:** $\text{circle centre }O,\ \text{radius }OA$

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C23", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "C-TANGENT", "diagram": "CON-TANGENT-01", "kind": "construction", "verification": "logic-review", "steps": ["MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3", "MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIB-Q08"} -->
**Topic:** Constructions · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `C-TANGENT`

**Question:** Draw a circle of radius 3 cm with centre O and mark a point A on it. Construct the tangent at A. Write the steps.

**Diagram:** `CON-TANGENT-01` → `Shared_Assets/Constructions/CON-TANGENT-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Tangent is perpendicular to the radius.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Draw the circle, radius 3 cm, and mark A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S1 -->

$$OA = r$$

Step 2 — Join OA and extend it beyond A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S2 -->

$$OA\ \text{extended}$$

Step 3 — Construct a perpendicular to OA at A. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S3 -->

$$\perp\ \text{at }A$$

Step 4 — That perpendicular is the tangent. <!-- MQ-G11C23-F-CONSTRUCT_-01-S4 -->

$$\text{tangent}\perp OA$$

**Final answer:** $\text{tangent at }A\perp OA$

<sub>Verification: logic-reviewed (teacher review recommended)</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C24", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "kind": "concept", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S1", "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S2", "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q23"} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION`

**Question:** U = {1, 2, …, 9}, A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}, B = {4, 5, 6, 7}. Find A ∩ B, A ∪ B and A′.

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN2-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN2-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** ∩ = and, ∪ = or, ′ = not.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Intersection: elements in both. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S1 -->

$$A\cap B = \{4,5\}$$

Step 2 — Union: elements in either. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S2 -->

$$A\cup B = \{1,2,3,4,5,6,7\}$$

Step 3 — Complement: in U but not in A. <!-- MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01-S3 -->

$$A' = \{6,7,8,9\}$$

**Final answer:** $A\cap B=\{4,5\};\ A\cup B=\{1,\dots,7\};\ A'=\{6,7,8,9\}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C24", "area": "SET", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION3", "diagram": "SET-VENN3-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S1", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S2", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S3", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S4", "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIIB-Q11"} -->
**Topic:** Sets · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-SET-UNION3`

**Question:** In a group of 50, 20 study Maths (M), 22 Science (S) and 18 English (E). 8 study M and S, 6 study S and E, 5 study M and E, and 3 study all three. Find (i) the number who study only Maths (ii) the number who study none.

**Diagram:** `SET-VENN3-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/SET-VENN3-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Always fill a Venn diagram from the centre outward.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Centre region (all three). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S1 -->

$$n(M\cap S\cap E) = 3$$

Step 2 — Exactly two subjects (subtract the centre). <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S2 -->

$$MS: 8-3=5,\ SE: 6-3=3,\ ME: 5-3=2$$

Step 3 — Only Maths. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S3 -->

$$20 - 5 - 2 - 3 = 10$$

Step 4 — Only S and only E similarly. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S4 -->

$$11,\ 10$$

Step 5 — None = total − all regions. <!-- MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01-S5 -->

$$50 - 44 = 6$$

**Final answer:** $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>


## G11C25 — Probability

### PP-OL2019-PIB-Q05
<!-- meta {"id": "PP-OL2019-PIB-Q05", "source": "OL2019", "source_type": "OFFICIAL", "exam_year": "2019", "medium": "English (source)", "paper": "I", "part": "B", "question": 5, "marks": 10, "chapter": "G11C25", "pattern": "P1B-PROB", "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT"} -->
- **Source:** G.C.E. (O/L) 2019 — Mathematics I & II (English medium) · Paper I · Part B · Q5 · 10 marks · [OL2019](https://e-thaksalawa.moe.gov.lk/lcms/pluginfile.php/46241/mod_resource/content/1/EM_Math_I%2CII%20pp_2019.PDF)

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S1", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S2", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S3", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S4", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIB-Q05"} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** Bag 1 has 2 red and 3 blue balls; Bag 2 has 1 red and 3 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Multiply along branches, add between outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — First branches (Bag 1). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S1 -->

$$P(R_1)=\frac{2}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{3}{5}$$

Step 2 — Second branches (Bag 2). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S2 -->

$$P(R_2)=\frac{1}{4},\ P(B_2)=\frac{3}{4}$$

Step 3 — Both red: multiply along the branch. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S3 -->

$$\frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{4} = \frac{1}{10}$$

Step 4 — Both blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S4 -->

$$\frac{3}{5}\times\frac{3}{4} = \frac{9}{20}$$

Step 5 — Same colour: add the two outcomes. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-01-S5 -->

$$\frac{1}{10} + \frac{9}{20} = \frac{11}{20}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{11}{20}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{11}{20}$

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02", "grade": 11, "chapter": "G11C25", "area": "PRO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S1", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S2", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S3", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S4", "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S5"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2018-PIB-Q04"} -->
**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** Bag 1 has 3 red and 2 blue balls; Bag 2 has 3 red and 1 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Multiply along branches, add between outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — First branches (Bag 1). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S1 -->

$$P(R_1)=\frac{3}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{2}{5}$$

Step 2 — Second branches (Bag 2). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S2 -->

$$P(R_2)=\frac{3}{4},\ P(B_2)=\frac{1}{4}$$

Step 3 — Both red: multiply along the branch. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S3 -->

$$\frac{3}{5}\times\frac{3}{4} = \frac{9}{20}$$

Step 4 — Both blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S4 -->

$$\frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{4} = \frac{1}{10}$$

Step 5 — Same colour: add the two outcomes. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02-S5 -->

$$\frac{9}{20} + \frac{1}{10} = \frac{11}{20}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{11}{20}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{11}{20}$

**Topic:** Probability · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `F-PRO-TREE`

**Question:** Bag 1 has 3 red and 2 blue balls; Bag 2 has 2 red and 2 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

**Diagram:** `PRO-TREE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Other_Diagrams/PRO-TREE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** Multiply along branches, add between outcomes.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — First branches (Bag 1). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03-S1 -->

$$P(R_1)=\frac{3}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{2}{5}$$

Step 2 — Second branches (Bag 2). <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03-S2 -->

$$P(R_2)=\frac{1}{2},\ P(B_2)=\frac{1}{2}$$

Step 3 — Both red: multiply along the branch. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03-S3 -->

$$\frac{3}{5}\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{3}{10}$$

Step 4 — Both blue. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03-S4 -->

$$\frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{1}{5}$$

Step 5 — Same colour: add the two outcomes. <!-- MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03-S5 -->

$$\frac{3}{10} + \frac{1}{5} = \frac{1}{2}$$

**Final answer:** $\frac{1}{2}$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $\frac{1}{2}$

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-TRISUM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S1", "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S2", "MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S3"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q03"} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** Foundation Mastery · **Difficulty:** easy · **Formula:** `T-GEO-TRISUM`

**Question:** Two angles of a triangle are 50° and 60°. Find the third angle.

- **Hint 1:** Sum of angles in a triangle = 180°.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Angles of a triangle add up to 180°. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S1 -->

$$x + 50 + 60 = 180$$

Step 2 — Add the known angles. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S2 -->

$$x + 110 = 180$$

Step 3 — Subtract. <!-- MQ-G10C08-F-ANGLE_SUM-04-S3 -->

$$x = 180 - 110 = 70^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $70^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $70^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-EXT", "diagram": "GEO-EXTANGLE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S1", "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-OL2019-PIA-Q21"} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-EXT`

**Question:** In ΔABC, BC is produced to D. ∠BAC = 55° and ∠ABC = 80°. Find ∠ACD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-EXTANGLE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-EXTANGLE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** ∠ACD is outside the triangle at C.
- **Hint 2:** Use the exterior angle theorem.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Exterior angle = sum of the two interior opposite angles. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S1 -->

$$\angle ACD = \angle BAC + \angle ABC$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-01-S2 -->

$$\angle ACD = 55^\circ + 80^\circ = 135^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $135^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $135^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

<sub>Verification: machine-checked</sub>

<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-04", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-EXT", "diagram": "GEO-EXTANGLE-01", "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-04-S1", "MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-04-S2"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q02"} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** Grade Improvement · **Difficulty:** medium · **Formula:** `T-GEO-EXT`

**Question:** In ΔABC, BC is produced to D. ∠BAC = 27° and ∠ABC = 96°. Find ∠ACD.

**Diagram:** `GEO-EXTANGLE-01` → `Shared_Assets/Geometry/GEO-EXTANGLE-01.svg` (generic diagram — not drawn to scale; use the values in the question)

- **Hint 1:** ∠ACD is outside the triangle at C.
- **Hint 2:** Use the exterior angle theorem.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Exterior angle = sum of the two interior opposite angles. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-04-S1 -->

$$\angle ACD = \angle BAC + \angle ABC$$

Step 2 — Substitute. <!-- MQ-G10C08-G-EXTERIOR_A-04-S2 -->

$$\angle ACD = 27^\circ + 96^\circ = 123^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $123^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $123^\circ$ · **Unit:** °

#### ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT — MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01", "grade": 10, "chapter": "G10C08", "area": "GEO", "medium": "en", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "T-GEO-TRISUM", "diagram": null, "kind": "numeric", "verification": "machine-checked", "steps": ["MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S1", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S2", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S3", "MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S4"], "answer_status": "ORIGINAL_EQUIVALENT", "linked_past_question": "PP-MOE2026-PIA-Q13"} -->
**Topic:** Triangles I · **Pathway:** A Grade Mastery · **Difficulty:** hard · **Formula:** `T-GEO-TRISUM`

**Question:** The angles of a triangle are $(2x + 10)^\circ$, $x^\circ$ and $95^\circ$. Find x and the largest angle.

- **Hint 1:** Angle sum = 180° gives an equation.
- **Hint 2:** After finding x, substitute back.

**Step-by-step solution**

Step 1 — Write the angle-sum equation. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S1 -->

$$(2x+10) + x + 95 = 180$$

Step 2 — Collect terms. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S2 -->

$$3x + 105 = 180$$

Step 3 — Solve. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S3 -->

$$3x = 75 \Rightarrow x = 25$$

Step 4 — Find each angle and pick the largest. <!-- MQ-G10C08-A-ANGLE_ALGE-01-S4 -->

$$2x+10 = 60^\circ,\ x = 25^\circ,\ 95^\circ \Rightarrow \max = 95^\circ$$

**Final answer:** $x=25,\ \text{largest}=95^\circ$
<br>**Accepted answer formats:** $x=25,\ \text{largest}=95^\circ$

> ⚠️ **Common mistake:** Giving x as the final answer when the largest angle is asked.
